user
Design discussion (read-only, no code changes; use a PTY if non-PTY shell returns blank stdout).
Context: pyfin de-novo candidate pipeline. We are redefining the CLUSTERING step (within one interval)
to be a clean standalone step. Relevant code you can re-read: fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py
(cluster_read_chains + _wobble/_cassette/_contains/_related + the mono handling + fold), and the
existing post-EM mono resolver fin/scoring/m2_junction_nll.py (mono_resolve_drops) + its wiring
(fin/pipeline/selection.py select_m2_interval, config mono_resolve_post_em default False).

The user has decided three things about the clustering step. Give your independent take on each —
agree / disagree / better proposal — with concrete reasoning and any risks. Be decisive.

DECISION 1: Do NOT fold in the clustering step. Clustering should ONLY group reads/structures into
families. The exact-sub-chain fold (and span-guard, and mono-fold) currently done inside
cluster_read_chains should move to a LATER per-cluster step. Agree? Any reason fold must stay at
cluster time?

DECISION 2: Accept the current single-linkage (transitive) union-find for now (A~B, B~C merges A,B,C
even if A not~ C). Rationale: in the target design a cluster is only a scoping unit for a shared read
pool; the distinct structural variants are KEPT as separate candidates and the final weighted
assignment decides read ownership, so an over-wide cluster is low-harm. Do you agree single-linkage is
acceptable here, or is there a cheap materially-better option (complete-linkage / representative-
centroid / merge-only-if-within-tol-of-cluster-rep)? When would single-linkage actually bite given
variants are kept + reads assigned by evidence?

DECISION 3 (the main one — critique this): mono-exon (single-exon, chain-less) reads should get NO
processing during clustering. They all become ONE separate "mono-cluster" for the interval (a holding
bucket). Only AFTER the multi-exon candidates are fully resolved (through EM + selection, i.e. we know
the FINAL surviving multi candidates) do we decide, per mono read: is it a genuine intronless
transcript, or just a 5'/3' fragment lying inside some surviving multi-exon candidate's exon?
Evaluate:
  (a) Is deferring mono resolution to AFTER multi-exon finalization correct in principle (you cannot
      classify a mono fragment without the final multi exon coordinates)? Does it align with the
      existing mono_resolve_post_em?
  (b) Risks: real intronless genes (histones, some lncRNAs) must survive — how to protect recall?
      One interval can span multiple loci, so the single mono-bucket pools unrelated single-exon reads
      — is that OK as a holding bucket, and what does the final decision step need (locus split?
      containment test vs surviving multi exons? read-support/length floor? weighting for a mono read
      contained in several multi candidates)?
  (c) EM interaction: if mono reads are held OUT of the multi-exon EM entirely, is that better or worse
      than including them? (A single-exon fragment inside a shared exon cannot discriminate between
      multi isoforms, so does holding it out remove noise or lose signal?) When a mono fragment is
      later folded into a surviving multi candidate, its reads/abundance are added post-hoc — is that
      sound for counting and for the reported abundance?
  (d) Any better structuring of the mono lifecycle than "one bucket now, decide at the very end"?

Keep it structured, decisive, concrete. Do not modify code.

codex
The code confirms post-EM mono resolution already uses the intended “surviving multis only” model, but it currently operates on mono candidates produced earlier, not on a raw interval-level holding bucket. I’m checking how cluster outputs become candidates so the recommendation accounts for lifecycle changes.
codex
Candidate creation already splits the single empty-chain “mono” member into spatial groups before emission, which is a useful clue: the new mono bucket can be coarse internally, but the final resolver still needs spatial/locus structure before it emits or drops mono transcripts.
codex
For the EM side: `m2_em` assigns over the whole candidate set and all interval reads, then selection mutates the resulting QuantResults. That makes mono inclusion/holding-out a real algorithmic choice, not just a presentation detail.
codex
The current global filters exempt mono candidates from most multi-exon survival filters, while the optional mono support gate and polyA gate are the main recall/precision controls for true intronless calls. That matters for protecting histone/lncRNA-style cases under a deferred design.

366-def support_gate_drops(cands, ties_by_read, nlls_by_read, tie_ok: bool = True):
367-    """Columns to drop by the M2/M1 read-support gate.
368-
369-    A multi-exon candidate (any source except fusion; mono-exon exempt) is KEPT
370-    iff it earns >=1 read's support:
371-      (a) it is some read's M1 SOLE best-AS — the read's AS tie set is exactly
372-          this one candidate (unique mappy-AS winner), OR
373-      (b) it is some read's M2-best — lowest junction-NLL among that read's
374-          scored tie candidates (``tie_ok`` True counts ties for the min;
375-          False requires a strict unique minimum).
376-    Otherwise the candidate is dropped. A jitter-corrupted GTF junction wins
377-    neither (it loses the M2 signal contest to the true-junction candidate and
378-    never holds a read's sole AS), whereas a genuine isoform always earns one or
379-    the other — so this drops corrupted-annotation shadows without an annotation
380-    oracle.
381-
382-    Args:
383-        cands: list of TranscriptCandidate (column order = list/index order).
384-        ties_by_read: read index -> list of candidate columns in its AS tie set.
385-        nlls_by_read: read index -> {candidate column -> junction NLL}.
386-        tie_ok: count candidates tied for the lowest NLL as M2-best (recall-safe).
387-
388-    Returns:
389-        set of candidate columns to drop.
390-    """
391-    # No support evidence at all (e.g. no signal backend AND the diff-cover gate
392-    # off, so neither ties nor NLLs were populated): the gate has nothing to judge
393-    # on, so it must NOT drop anything (dropping every candidate would be wrong).
394-    if not ties_by_read and not nlls_by_read:
395-        return set()
396-    supported: set = set()
397-    for tie in ties_by_read.values():
398-        if tie is not None and len(tie) == 1:
399-            supported.add(tie[0])
400-    for nlls in nlls_by_read.values():
401-        if not nlls:
402-            continue
403-        best = min(nlls.values())
404-        winners = [j for j, v in nlls.items() if v <= best]
405-        if tie_ok:
406-            supported.update(winners)
407-        elif len(winners) == 1:
408-            supported.add(winners[0])

fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py
1-"""Generation-side intron-chain clustering.
2-
3-Cluster-first candidate generation. Pool all reads, group by their EXACT (raw,
4-un-snapped) intron chain, then:
5-
6-  1. COLLAPSE exact sub-chains  -- a chain that is a strict contiguous sub-chain of a
7-     longer chain with IDENTICAL coordinates folds into it (reads pooled, chain gone).
8-  2. CLUSTER the survivors      -- union-find groups the remaining distinct chains into
9-     one cluster when related by any of:
10-       * WOBBLE      : same intron count, every junction within ``wobble_bp``,
11-       * CASSETTE    : K vs K-1 chains differing by one small (< ``cassette_max_exon_bp``)
12-                       skipped exon (minimap2's small-exon-skip artefact),
13-       * CONTAINMENT : a shorter chain that is a contiguous sub-chain of a longer one
14-                       within ``wobble_bp`` per junction (the non-exact leftovers that
15-                       step 1 did not fold).
16-
17-A cluster KEEPS all its member candidates (distinct chains + pooled reads); only exact
18-sub-chains disappear. NO consensus snapping (coordinates are never rewritten). The
19-cluster is the downstream unit: reads attach to a cluster instead of splitting across
20-near-duplicates, and which members are genuine isoforms is decided later by the
21-per-cluster computation. The caller runs with canonical_search_bp = 0 (no shadows).
22-
23-Mono-exon chains never join a structural cluster; each unique mono chain is a singleton.
24-"""
25-from __future__ import annotations
26-
27-from dataclasses import dataclass, field
28-from typing import Dict, List, Set, Tuple

fin/candidates/isoform_recovery.py
1-"""5'-TSS short-isoform recovery (post-EM).
2-
3-Generation folds every exact contiguous sub-chain into its container, so a genuine
4-SHORT isoform that is an exact 5'-truncation of a longer one disappears as a candidate
5-and its reads are pooled into the long member. This module brings it back AFTER the
6:per-cluster EM has confirmed the long member: the folded sub-chains are dormant SHADOW
7:proposals (``GenCandidate.folded``); each proposes a 5' transcription start (its
8-truncation boundary). We test each proposal against the long member's read 5'-end
9-distribution and activate only those that show a SHARP peak -- a real TSS -- rather than
10-the smooth ramp of dRNA 5'-degradation.
11-
12-The test is deliberately conservative and signal-free (structure + read-end pileup only,
13-per the validated 5'-TSS-pileup finding). Direct peak-excess support only; a full
14-degradation/abundance model is deferred. Only 5' TSS peaks for now (dRNA's dominant
15-gap); 3' polyA recovery is a later addition.
16-"""
17-from __future__ import annotations
18-
19-from dataclasses import dataclass
20-from typing import List, Sequence, Tuple
21-
22-
23-@dataclass
24-class RecoveredPeak:
25-    """An accepted 5'-TSS proposal: the peak position and its read support."""
26-    pos: int            # genomic 5' TSS position (the proposal that passed)
27-    support: float      # EM-weighted reads whose 5' end falls in +-window of pos
28-    excess: float       # support minus the local degradation background (peak excess)
29-    oe: float           # observed / expected (enrichment over flank background)
30-    concentration: float  # fraction of +-(flank_mult*w) reads that lie in +-w
31-
32-
33-def _weighted_in(ends: Sequence[Tuple[int, float]], center: int, half: int) -> float:
34-    """Total weight of 5'-end positions within ``+-half`` of ``center``."""
35-    tot = 0.0
36-    for pos, w in ends:
37-        if abs(pos - center) <= half:
38-            tot += w
39-    return tot
40-
41-
42-def evaluate_tss_peak(
43-    ends: Sequence[Tuple[int, float]],
44-    pos: int,
45-    *,
46-    window: int = 20,
47-    flank_mult: int = 3,
48-    min_oe: float = 4.0,
49-    min_concentration: float = 0.6,
50-    min_reads: float = 8.0,
51-    min_frac: float = 0.03,
52-    total_mass: float = 0.0,
53-) -> RecoveredPeak | None:
54-    """Peak-vs-ramp test for one proposed 5'-TSS position.
55-
56-    ``ends`` are (genomic 5'-end position, EM weight) for the containment family's
57-    reads. A proposal at ``pos`` is accepted only if the read-end pileup in ``+-window``
58-    is a sharp peak over the local degradation background (estimated from the flanks
59-    ``window < |d| <= flank_mult*window``), meeting ALL of:
60-      * support (weight in +-window) >= max(``min_reads``, ``min_frac`` * family mass),
61-      * enrichment O/E >= ``min_oe`` (observed vs flank-density extrapolation),
62-      * concentration >= ``min_concentration`` (share of +-(flank_mult*w) inside +-w).
63-    Returns a :class:`RecoveredPeak` (with peak-excess support) or None if rejected.
64-
65-    Flank-based background is direction-agnostic (a real TSS stands above the local
66-    ramp on either side), so no strand handling is needed here.
67-    """
68-    w_in = _weighted_in(ends, pos, window)
69-    w_wide = _weighted_in(ends, pos, flank_mult * window)
70-    w_flank = w_wide - w_in
71-    flank_bp = 2 * (flank_mult - 1) * window            # total flank width (bp)
72-    bg_density = (w_flank / flank_bp) if flank_bp > 0 else 0.0
73-    expected = bg_density * (2 * window)                # background reads in +-window
74-    oe = (w_in / expected) if expected > 0 else float("inf")
75-    concentration = (w_in / w_wide) if w_wide > 0 else 0.0
76-    support_floor = max(min_reads, min_frac * total_mass)
77-    if w_in >= support_floor and oe >= min_oe and concentration >= min_concentration:
78-        return RecoveredPeak(
79-            pos=pos, support=w_in, excess=max(0.0, w_in - expected),
80-            oe=oe, concentration=concentration)
81-    return None
82-
83-
84-def recover_5p_peaks(
85-    ends: Sequence[Tuple[int, float]],
86-    proposals: Sequence[int],
87-    *,
88-    window: int = 20,
89-    flank_mult: int = 3,
90-    min_oe: float = 4.0,
91-    min_concentration: float = 0.6,
92-    min_reads: float = 8.0,
93-    min_frac: float = 0.03,
94-    merge_bp: int = 20,
95-) -> List[RecoveredPeak]:
96-    """Test every candidate 5'-TSS ``proposals`` against the read-end pileup ``ends``.
97-
98:    ``proposals`` are candidate genomic TSS positions (from folded shadow boundaries).
99-    Returns the accepted peaks, de-duplicated: proposals within ``merge_bp`` collapse
100-    to the single strongest (highest support) peak, so a nested short isoform is not
101-    counted under several near-identical proposals. Deterministic (proposals are sorted;
102-    ties break on position).
103-    """
104-    total_mass = sum(w for _, w in ends)
105-    hits: List[RecoveredPeak] = []
106-    for p in sorted(set(proposals)):
107-        r = evaluate_tss_peak(
108-            ends, p, window=window, flank_mult=flank_mult, min_oe=min_oe,
109-            min_concentration=min_concentration, min_reads=min_reads,
110-            min_frac=min_frac, total_mass=total_mass)
111-        if r is not None:
112-            hits.append(r)
113-    # merge near-duplicate accepted peaks (keep the strongest, then lowest pos).
114-    hits.sort(key=lambda r: (-r.support, r.pos))
115-    kept: List[RecoveredPeak] = []
116-    for r in hits:
117-        if all(abs(r.pos - k.pos) > merge_bp for k in kept):
118-            kept.append(r)
119-    kept.sort(key=lambda r: r.pos)
120-    return kept

666-def _wobble_collapse_novel(
667-    novel: List[TranscriptCandidate], threshold: int, tol: int,

669-) -> List[TranscriptCandidate]:
670-    """Greedy consensus merge of novel candidates that differ only by junction
671-    wobble (each junction within ``tol`` bp) AND share a 3' end within
672-    ``threshold``. The HIGHEST-read-support candidate is the consensus
673-    representative (the reads' own mode); wobbled shadows union their reads into
674-    it and vanish. Deterministic order: (-support, -length, start, 3', end, chain).
675-
676-    ``shadow_ratio`` guards against merging genuine CLOSE isoforms (real
677-    alternative donor/acceptor sites a few bp apart, e.g. NAGNAG): a candidate is
678-    absorbed into a rep only if it is a true SHADOW — ``len(cand.reads) <=
679-    shadow_ratio * len(rep.reads)`` (rep always has >= support, given the sort).
680-    A minimap wobble shadow has few reads; a real close isoform has comparable
681-    support and stays separate. isoquant's bulge-removal uses the ½ analogue.
682-    ``shadow_ratio >= 1.0`` merges any wobble-match (no isoform protection).
683-
684-    NOT annotation-snap: the consensus coordinate comes from the most-supported
685-    READS, never a reference. Merges shadows BEFORE they exist as separate
686-    candidates (reads never split-then-dropped — avoids the read-reassignment
687-    trap that made the post-hoc dominance filter backfire).
688-    """

692-    # clustering deterministic regardless of the candidates' input order.
693-    # Total order (candidate_id last makes it strict, so representative choice
694-    # and clustering are fully input-order-independent).
695-    order = sorted(

697:        key=lambda c: (-len(c.supporting_read_ids), -c.length, c.start,

137-def isoform_fraction_drops(
138-    results: Dict[str, QuantResult],
139-    min_fraction: float,
140-) -> set:
141-    """Return candidate_ids to drop by the minimum-isoform-fraction heuristic.
142-
143-    For every NOVEL multi-exon transcript C, compute its locus-relative
144:    abundance::
145-
146:        relabund(C) = C.abundance / max(abundance over ALL transcripts
147-                       (novel, GTF and fusion) that OVERLAP C, C included)
148-
149-    and drop C when ``relabund(C) < min_fraction``. This is the standard
150-    Cufflinks ``--min-isoform-fraction`` / StringTie ``-f`` minor-isoform
151-    suppression rule: the low-fraction tail of a locus is enriched for
152-    incompletely-spliced precursors (pre-mRNA), RT/template-switching
153-    artifacts, and assembly noise rather than genuine isoforms.
154-
155-    The locus maximum spans ALL sources (not just novel): on dense annotated
156-    loci the dominant isoform is usually a GTF-passthrough transcript, so
157-    comparing a novel only against other novels lets wobble-shadow isoforms
158-    (junction-shifted near-copies of a true transcript) escape — they only
159-    compete with each other and keep a deceptively high novel-only fraction.
160-    Measuring each novel against the strongest transcript of ANY source at its
161-    locus collapses those shadows below the threshold.
162-
163-    GTF-passthrough (``source="gtf"``), fusion (``source="fusion"``), and
164-    single-exon (mono) candidates are EXEMPT and never dropped — they only ever
165-    contribute to the locus maximum, never to the drop set. ``min_fraction
166-    <= 0`` disables the filter (returns an empty set).
167-
168:    SIRV WARNING: synthetic SIRV has no genuine low-abundance isoform tail, so
169-    its F1-optimal threshold (~0.4) is 4-40x more aggressive than real tools
170-    and would gut recall on a real transcriptome. Keep this conservative
171-    (literature default 0.01) and re-tune on real data.
172-    """
173-    if min_fraction <= 0.0:
174-        return set()
175-    # Bucket by (chrom, strand): only candidates on the same chromosome and
176-    # strand can share a locus, so the pairwise overlap scan stays within a
177-    # bucket instead of running all-vs-all across the whole genome. (Within a
178-    # locus-dense single chrom/strand it is still O(n^2); a sweep-line could be
179-    # added if a real run ever makes that the bottleneck.)
180-    #
181-    # The bucket holds ALL candidates (so the locus maximum can come from a GTF
182-    # or fusion transcript); only NOVEL multi-exon candidates are eligible to be
183-    # dropped.
184-    buckets: Dict[Tuple[str, str], List[QuantResult]] = defaultdict(list)
185-    for qr in results.values():
186-        buckets[(qr.chrom, qr.strand)].append(qr)
187-    drops: set = set()
188-    for bucket in buckets.values():
189-        for qr in bucket:
190-            # Only novel multi-exon candidates are droppable; GTF/fusion/mono
191-            # only ever serve as locus-maximum competitors.
192-            if qr.source != "novel" or len(qr.exons) < 2:
193-                continue
194:            locus_max = qr.abundance
195-            for other in bucket:
196-                if other.candidate_id != qr.candidate_id and _qr_overlap(qr, other):
197:                    if other.abundance > locus_max:
198:                        locus_max = other.abundance
199-            # The locus-dominant isoform (relabund == 1.0) is NEVER dropped,
200-            # even if a caller mis-configures min_fraction > 1.0.
201:            if qr.abundance >= locus_max:
202-                continue
203:            if locus_max > 0 and (qr.abundance / locus_max) < min_fraction:
204-                drops.add(qr.candidate_id)
205-    return drops

208-def soft_mass_ratio_drops(
209-    results: Dict[str, QuantResult],
210-    max_ratio: float,
211-) -> set:
212-    """Return candidate_ids to drop by the soft-mass / hard-read ratio test.
213-
214:    For a NOVEL multi-exon candidate C, compare its EM SOFT abundance
215-    (``R.sum`` = fractional probability mass) against its HARD read count
216:    (``num_assigned_reads`` = number of reads whose argmax is C)::
217-
218:        ratio(C) = C.abundance / C.num_assigned_reads
219-
220-    and drop C when ``ratio >= max_ratio`` (a candidate with zero hard reads is
221-    always dropped — it lives entirely on borrowed soft mass).
222-
223-    A genuine isoform's reads argmax to it AND deposit their full soft mass on
224-    it, so ratio ~= 1. A wobble shadow steals little hard support but, being a
225:    +-bp structural near-copy of a high-abundance anchor, accumulates fractional
226:    soft crumbs from every one of the anchor's reads -> its soft abundance is
227-    inflated far above its honest hard-read count (ratio >> 1). This catches the
228:    HIGH-abundance shadows that the locus-relative ``isoform_fraction`` /
229-    cluster-recheck levers cannot (a shadow at 32% of the anchor passes a
230-    fraction gate but still shows an inflated soft/hard ratio). Pure EM evidence
231-    — no GTF, no annotation oracle.
232-
233-    GTF-passthrough (``source="gtf"``), fusion (``source="fusion"``), and
234-    single-exon (mono) candidates are EXEMPT. ``max_ratio <= 0`` disables the
235-    filter (returns an empty set).
236-
237-    SIRV WARNING: the separating threshold is data-dependent (true isoforms
238-    cluster at ratio ~1 but a few reach ~1.4-1.6 when their own reads are
239-    genuinely ambiguous); tune on real data before trusting an aggressive value.
240-    """
241-    if max_ratio <= 0.0:
242-        return set()
243-    drops: set = set()
244-    for qr in results.values():
245-        if qr.source != "novel" or len(qr.exons) < 2:
246-            continue
247:        if qr.num_assigned_reads <= 0:
248-            drops.add(qr.candidate_id)
249-            continue
250:        if (qr.abundance / qr.num_assigned_reads) >= max_ratio:
251-            drops.add(qr.candidate_id)
252-    return drops

255-def mono_exon_drops(

314-318-    # shadow is dropped. Parent may be gtf or novel; a gtf candidate is NEVER
320-    # intron equality on the shared suffix) so genuine exon-skipping / alt-3'-end
    # alt-TSS isoform that starts at a downstream exon and shares the 3' end, so it
324-    # Fold the shadow only when its EM abundance <= parent's * this ratio (shadow
329-    # containment_cluster_wobble_bp per junction) of a longer candidate — the
334-    # truncation / exon-skip shadow the same-intron-count wobble cluster never groups
335-    # — ONLY when it is a low-support shadow by BOTH EM abundance (<= parent *
336-
339-    # reads comparable to the parent (median 13), so requiring BOTH keeps MOST real short
340-    # isoforms — but a genuine low-fraction minor isoform (e.g. parent 100 reads, real
341-    # 20-read isoform <30% abundance) can still be dropped, so this is recall-safER, not
342-    # recall-safe. DEFAULT-ON (production): a targeted, read-guarded precision lever that
343-    # drops truncation/exon-skip mapping shadows the wobble cluster misses. Validated on
344-    # the gencode sweep (p00 honestF1 +1.0 at <=0.5 corrRec cost; c_jitter/full
345-    # non-regressing in the containment-only ablation). The absolute cap
346-    # (containment_cluster_max_shadow_reads) bounds the residual recall risk.
347-    containment_cluster: bool = True
348-    containment_cluster_wobble_bp: int = 6
349-    containment_cluster_min_ab_ratio: float = 0.3
350-    containment_cluster_min_read_ratio: float = 0.3
351-    # absolute cap: never drop a shadow carrying more than this many supporting reads,
352-    # regardless of the ratio (a real low-fraction isoform of a very-high-support parent
353-    # can exceed min_read_ratio*parent). 0 disables (ratio-only). 10 keeps the validated
354-    # benefit (shadows are ~1-3 reads) while never folding a >10-read candidate.
355-    containment_cluster_max_shadow_reads: int = 10
356-    # Lever 3 — mono-exon (single-exon) read-support gate (post-aggregate, in
357-    # _finalize_and_write). Drop a NOVEL single-exon candidate whose hard read
358:    # count < min_mono_exon_reads OR genomic length < min_mono_exon_length.
359-    # Suppresses single-exon de novo noise (IsoQuant drops novel unspliced by
360-    # default for ONT) WITHOUT a blanket hard drop: a high-support / long real
361-    # intronless gene (histone, many ncRNAs) survives. gtf/fusion/multi-exon are
362-    # EXEMPT (only novel mono are gated). Master switch drop_mono_exon_novel must
363-    # be True AND at least one threshold > 0 to fire; otherwise no drops
364-    # (byte-identical). Defaults OFF.
365-    drop_mono_exon_novel: bool = False
366:    min_mono_exon_reads: int = 0
367:    min_mono_exon_length: int = 0
368-    # Lever 2 — per-junction read-support gate (quant_mode="m2_em" only). Drop a
369-    # NOVEL multi-exon candidate if ANY of its junctions is spliced by fewer than
370-    # novel_junction_min_reads directly-observed reads (intron junctions extracted
371-    # from primary-read CIGARs, strand-keyed, matched within novel_junction_reads
372-    # _tol bp). I.e. a novel junction must be carried by >= N independent reads,
373-    # not just 1. gtf/fusion/mono are EXEMPT (only novel multi-exon are gated).
374-    # <= 1 disables (every assembled junction trivially has >=1 read).
375-    # DEFAULT 2 (production, landed after the full off-vs-on sweep): a novel
376-    # junction must be carried by >= 2 reads. On real human GENCODE it lifts
377-    # precision at ZERO recall cost across EVERY scenario — de novo Pr +3.8,
378-    # p10 +3.2, p50 +1.5, p90/full +0.8, and all six corrupted-GTF ratios
379-    # (c_jitter/skip/flip/merge/spurious/ir) +0.6..+0.9 — and keeps pyfin #1 on
380-    # SIRV4 (F1@3 85.0, wash). NOTE: on SYNTHETIC de novo / aggressive-corruption
381-    # this trades ~0.4-0.8 F1@3 (recall) for precision, but the real-data arbiter
382-    # and #1 standing justify it. Set 0 to disable.
383-    novel_junction_min_reads: int = 2
384-    novel_junction_reads_tol: int = 2
385-    # DE-NOVO wobble-tolerant collapse (EXPERIMENT default-OFF). At discovery,
386-    # novel candidates are bucketed by EXACT intron chain, so a junction at
387-    # (1000,2000) and a minimap-wobbled (1002,1998) become SEPARATE candidates
388-    # (wobble shadows) — the measured cause of pyfin's low de-novo structural
389-    # precision (p00 65.6% vs isoquant 91.3%). When > 0, _collapse_candidates
390-    # merges novel candidates whose intron chains match within this many bp per
391-    # junction (AND 3' within three_prime_threshold) into the highest-read-support
392-    # representative (consensus of the reads' OWN mode — NOT annotation-snap). 0
393-    # disables (byte-identical). isoquant uses Δ=6 for ONT.
394-    denovo_wobble_tol: int = 0
395-    # Shadow-ratio guard for the wobble merge: absorb a wobble-matching novel
396-    # candidate into a rep ONLY if it is a true SHADOW — len(cand.reads) <=
397-    # ratio * len(rep.reads). Protects genuine CLOSE isoforms (real alt
398-    # donor/acceptor a few bp apart, e.g. NAGNAG) from being merged: a minimap
399-    # shadow has few reads, a real close isoform has comparable support. 1.0
400-    # merges any wobble-match (naive; measured to eat correct transcripts at
401-    # tol=6). isoquant's ½-support bulge criterion ≈ 0.5. Only used when
402-    # denovo_wobble_tol > 0.
403-    denovo_wobble_shadow_ratio: float = 0.5
404-    # DE-NOVO intron-graph assembly (EXPERIMENT default-OFF). Attacks the measured
405-    # #1 de-novo error: truncation (dRNA 3'-bias -> reads cover only 3' junctions
406-    # -> pyfin emits truncated candidates, gffcompare class 'c'). When ON, pool all
407-    # reads' junctions, cluster wobbles to a read-count consensus (denovo_graph_tol
408-    # bp), build a read-adjacency graph, and EXTEND each read's chain through
409-    # UNAMBIGUOUSLY-supported edges (>= denovo_graph_min_edge_reads) to a maximal
410-    # chain — assembling truncated partials into full-length transcripts, stopping
411-    # at genuine branch points (no fabricated wrong combos). Reads are then grouped
412-    # by the extended chain; the candidate span is the union of its reads' extents
413-    # (5'/3' ends) and its sequence is built from the genome. NOT annotation-snap
414-    # (consensus + edges from reads only). OFF => byte-identical.
415-    denovo_graph: bool = False
416-    denovo_graph_tol: int = 6
417-    denovo_graph_min_edge_reads: int = 2

Below this the read is redistributed by the covered-read prior (flat 1/K when no prior exists). SIRV-tuned; sweep on real data.")
62-@click.option("--m2-cluster-recheck/--no-m2-cluster-recheck", "m2_cluster_recheck", default=True, show_default=True, help="(--quant-mode m2_em only) After EM, cluster ALL multi-exon candidates by structure (same intron count + every junction within --m2-cluster-recheck-bp); within a cluster the highest-abundance candidate anchors (often the true isoform / a GTF passthrough) and a NOVEL sibling whose abundance < --m2-cluster-recheck-fraction of the anchor is dropped as a wobble shadow (GTF/fusion never dropped). Pure abundance evidence — GTF only joins the abundance race, never used as a correctness oracle, so it stays robust on corrupted/absent annotation. Default ON; --no-m2-cluster-recheck disables (no drops).")

fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py
1-"""Generation-side intron-chain clustering.
2-
3-Cluster-first candidate generation. Pool all reads, group by their EXACT (raw,
4-un-snapped) intron chain, then:
5-
6-  1. COLLAPSE exact sub-chains  -- a chain that is a strict contiguous sub-chain of a
7-     longer chain with IDENTICAL coordinates folds into it (reads pooled, chain gone).
8-  2. CLUSTER the survivors      -- union-find groups the remaining distinct chains into
9-     one cluster when related by any of:
10-       * WOBBLE      : same intron count, every junction within ``wobble_bp``,
11-       * CASSETTE    : K vs K-1 chains differing by one small (< ``cassette_max_exon_bp``)
12-                       skipped exon (minimap2's small-exon-skip artefact),
13-       * CONTAINMENT : a shorter chain that is a contiguous sub-chain of a longer one
14-                       within ``wobble_bp`` per junction (the non-exact leftovers that
15-                       step 1 did not fold).
16-
17-A cluster KEEPS all its member candidates (distinct chains + pooled reads); only exact
18-sub-chains disappear. NO consensus snapping (coordinates are never rewritten). The
19-cluster is the downstream unit: reads attach to a cluster instead of splitting across
20-near-duplicates, and which members are genuine isoforms is decided later by the
21-per-cluster computation. The caller runs with canonical_search_bp = 0 (no shadows).
22-
23-Mono-exon chains never join a structural cluster; each unique mono chain is a singleton.
24-"""

1-"""Per-cluster read assignment (the "compute inside a cluster" stage of chain-cluster
2-generation).
3-
4-Given ONE generation cluster -- its member candidate chains and the reads pooled into
5-it -- decide, WITHOUT ever comparing a read to a candidate in another cluster:
6-
7-  1. Which member each read supports (read assignment), and
8-  2. Which members earn enough independent support to survive as real isoforms.
9-
10-The policy here is pure/deterministic and signal-agnostic; the two mechanisms it needs
11--- mappy alignment scores (M1) and the summed-LLR junction-signal tiebreak (M2) -- are
12-injected by the caller (the runner), so this module has no krill/mappy import and is
13-unit-testable on synthetic inputs.
14-
15-Assignment builds an M3 read x member compatibility matrix, then EM quantifies it:
16-  * M1 within-cluster: each read is scored ONLY against this cluster's members; its
17-    best-AS tie set is computed from those scores.
18-  * tie == 1  -> the read is COMPATIBLE with that one member (a certain anchor).
19-  * tie >= 2:
20-      - the read STRADDLES a junction that differs among the tied members AND M2
21-        (summed-LLR) returns a STRICT best (positive margin -- not an NLL tie)
22-        -> compatible with only that signal winner (a certain anchor). No margin
23-        threshold: any strict winner counts.
24-      - otherwise (no straddle, or M2 is an NLL tie / abstains) -> the read stays
25-        AMBIGUOUS: compatible with ALL its tied members (initial 1/k each).
26-  * EM: certain reads (compat set size 1) anchor member abundance every iteration;
27-    ambiguous reads redistribute among their tied members in proportion to the
28-    learned abundances (RSEM/kallisto-style). No hard "main peak" routing.
29-  * A member SURVIVES iff its EM abundance >= ``min_support``.
30-"""
31-from __future__ import annotations
32-
33-from dataclasses import dataclass, field
34-from typing import Callable, Dict, List, Optional, Sequence, Set, Tuple

36-Chain = Tuple[Tuple[int, int], ...]

40-# margin is the summed-LLR gap to the runner-up (> 0 == strict winner), or None to
41-# abstain. A non-positive margin is treated as "no strict winner" (read stays ambiguous).
42-M2Resolver = Callable[[str, List[int]], Optional[Tuple[int, float]]]

Targets the c_jitter precision collapse, and trims GTF echo in loci that DO show observed splicing (a FULLY read-sparse locus fails open — echo there is unaffected). NOT recall-safe (also drops low-coverage annotated junctions). Default 0 (OFF) pending real-data validation.")

Attacks pyfin's low de-novo structural precision (wobble shadows: a junction and its ±few-bp minimap variant currently survive as separate novel transcripts). 88-@click.option("--denovo-graph/--no-denovo-graph", "denovo_graph", default=False, show_default=True, help="(EXPERIMENT) De-novo intron-graph assembly: pool all reads' junctions, cluster wobbles to a read-count consensus (--denovo-graph-tol), build a read-adjacency graph, and EXTEND each read's chain through UNAMBIGUOUSLY-supported edges (>= --denovo-graph-min-edge-reads) into a maximal full-length chain — assembling truncated 3'-biased dRNA reads into full transcripts (attacks the measured #1 de-novo error: 'c' contained/truncated). If >= --denovo-graph-tss-frac of the reads starting at a chain's 5' junction pile their 5'-end within --denovo-graph-tss-tol bp (and >= --denovo-graph-tss-min-reads), that's a real TSS and the short isoform is kept instead of merged into the longer one. 14-    # Input files
15-    bam_path: str
16-    gtf_path: Optional[str] = None

27-    max_gap: int = 0
28-    min_novel_reads: int = 1          # A2: min supporting reads for a novel candidate (after collapsing)
29-    # NEW generation-side clustering (PRODUCTION DEFAULT). Group reads by intron chain
30-    # (3' ignored), collapse EXACT sub-chains, cluster wobble/cassette/containment
31-    # keeping members; NO canonical-search shadow generation. Replaces the legacy
32-    # 3'+exact-chain grouping + canonical expansion. On p00 (no gates, candidates = the
33-    # result) this cut raw candidates 175k->37k, c -73%, j -88%, structural precision
34-    # 10.1%->26.4% (2.6x), at a ~20% distinct-truth recall cost that is 92% attributable
35-    # to the exact sub-chain collapse (recoverable later by guarding the collapse).
36-    chain_cluster_discovery: bool = True
37-    chain_cluster_wobble_bp: int = 6
38-    chain_cluster_cassette_max_exon_bp: int = 70
39:    chain_cluster_fold_monoexon: bool = True  # fold single-exon reads wholly inside a multi-exon candidate's exon into that candidate (5'/3' degradation fragments) instead of emitting standalone mono candidates; intronic/uncontained mono reads stay separate. PRODUCTION DEFAULT (p00 m2_em: out 9581->9181, `=` 6323->6414, Pr 66.0->69.9 -- de-fragmentation both-axes win). --no- reverts to legacy standalone mono candidates.
40-    chain_cluster_fold_span_guard: bool = True  # only fold a read into an exact-sub-chain container if its aligned span does NOT run exonically across one of the container's EXTRA introns; a read spanning such an intron (retained-intron / alternative isoform) is kept as its own candidate instead of being absorbed. PRODUCTION DEFAULT. --no- reverts to unconditional exact-sub-chain fold.
41:    # Post-EM mono-exon resolution (quant_mode="m2_em" only). When True, single-exon
42-    # reads are NOT folded into multi-exon candidates at generation (chain_cluster_fold_

170-    # RSEM/Salmon-style iterative abundance feedback inside the EM (assignments.

172-    # vector theta from the current responsibilities and biases the energy by
173-    # -sigma*log(theta_j), so a read shared between candidates is pulled toward the
174-    # more abundant one (a 0.5/0.5 split migrating toward 0.99/0.01 once theta

183-    # Quantification engine selector (replaces the enable_signal/r1_variant/
184-    # r1_scoring triplet). The three modes are mutually exclusive:
185-    #   "argmax" : mappy AS argmax + M2 krill junction tiebreak, hard counts,
186-    #              NO EM. (M1-first; on SIRV peaks slightly above m2_em.)
187-    #   "m1_em"  : EM seeded by the M1 mappy AS-gap distance (beta=0, no signal
188-    #              coherence). Pure-alignment soft assignment.
189-    #   "m2_em"  : PRODUCTION DEFAULT. EM seeded by the PURE tie-break junction-NLL
190-    #              M2 distance: M1/AS picks each read's best-AS tie set + mappability
191-    #              mask, and the per-event junction-NLL is the sole graded distance
192-    #              over that tie set (m2_resolve_tie semantics, NOT the dense
193-    #              read×candidate matrix). Optionally adds the M3 read×read DTW
194-    #              coherence when m3_coherence=True (beta=em_beta). Chosen as the
195-    #              default for real-dRNA robustness (principled soft assignment);
196-    #              on SIRV it clears the competitor floor (Tx-F1 ~48.9 no-GTF /
197-    #              59.2 with-GTF, M3 off) just under argmax.

313-    # Lever 1 — containment / 5'-truncation collapse (quant_mode="m2_em" only).
314-    # Post-EM, fold a NOVEL candidate whose intron chain is a pure 3' SUFFIX of a
315-    # longer candidate (a 5'-truncation shadow: same downstream junctions, 3'
316-    # terminus within containment_3p_tol_bp, 5' end interior) into that longer
317-    # parent: the shadow's reads + soft mass are reassigned to the parent and the
318-    # shadow is dropped. Parent may be gtf or novel; a gtf candidate is NEVER
319-    # folded away (only novels are droppable). The suffix match is STRICT (exact
320-    # intron equality on the shared suffix) so genuine exon-skipping / alt-3'-end
321-    # isoforms (different splicing) are never folded. WARNING: this rule cannot
322-    # distinguish a dRNA 5'-truncation artifact from a genuine low-abundance
323-    # alt-TSS isoform that starts at a downstream exon and shares the 3' end, so it
324-    # is NOT recall-safe by construction; default OFF, enable only after real-truth

331-    # Containment-CLUSTER drop (recall-SAFER generalisation of containment_collapse):

334-    # truncation / exon-skip shadow the same-intron-count wobble cluster never groups

404-    # DE-NOVO intron-graph assembly (EXPERIMENT default-OFF). Attacks the measured

407-    # reads' junctions, cluster wobbles to a read-count consensus (denovo_graph_tol

409-    # UNAMBIGUOUSLY-supported edges (>= denovo_graph_min_edge_reads) to a maximal
410-    # chain — assembling truncated partials into full-length transcripts, stopping

412-    # by the extended chain; the candidate span is the union of its reads' extents

418-    # 5'-TSS brake for --denovo-graph. dRNA truncation is 5'-ward, so a truncated
419-    # read of a LONG transcript stops at a random 5' position while a COMPLETE read
420-    # of a genuine short isoform stops at that isoform's real TSS. A chain whose
421-    # 5'-terminal junction sits at a TSS peak (>= tss_frac of its reads pile their
422-    # 5'-end within tss_tol bp, and >= tss_min_reads reads) is NOT extended 5'-ward,
423-    # so short isoforms contained inside a longer transcript survive instead of

432-    # guided_junction_min_reads directly-observed reads (primary-read CIGAR introns,
433-    # strand-keyed, within guided_junction_reads_tol bp of BOTH boundaries). Novel
434:    # candidates keep going through Lever 2; fusion/mono are exempt.
435-    # WHY: Lever 2 + the coordinate-EXACT gates are novel-only, so a jitter-corrupted
436-    # GTF junction (coords shifted >tol from the true site) is never checked against
437-    # read CIGARs — it only faces the coordinate-INEXACT M2/M1 support gate, which a
438-    # ±10bp shift survives (it still holds reads' mappy sole-AS / ties the NLL). That
439-    # is the measured c_jitter precision collapse (94.8→41.3). Requiring exact read
440-    # support for GTF junctions attacks that directly; it also trims GTF echo
441-    # (orphan) in loci with observed splicing — but a FULLY read-sparse locus fails
442-    # open (observed map is None -> no drop), so echo there is unaffected.
443-    # RECALL RISK: this also drops genuine but low-coverage annotated junctions —
444-    # the classic real-vs-synthetic sign-flip knob. Keep min_reads small, tol tight,
445-    # and validate on real data across samples before any default flip. 0 disables
446-    # (byte-identical / recall-safe).
447-    guided_junction_min_reads: int = 0
448-    guided_junction_reads_tol: int = 2
449-    # Junction-dominance gate (PRE-EM, in process_interval after the canonical
450-    # gate). The "junction-first" idea: before quantification, drop a NOVEL
451-    # multi-exon candidate if ANY of its junctions is either (a) supported by
452-    # fewer than junction_dominance_min_reads directly-observed reads, OR (b) NOT
453-    # locally dominant — i.e. a DIFFERENT observed junction within
454-    # junction_dominance_window_bp (beyond junction_dominance_tol_bp) carries
455-    # strictly more reads. Unlike a pure read-count gate this also removes
456-    # multi-read wobble shadows (they lose to the stronger true junction a few bp
457-    # away). Pure mapping evidence (CIGAR introns, strand-keyed), no snap; runs
458:    # before EM so shadows never compete for reads. gtf/fusion/mono exempt.
459-    # OFF (default) -> no drops (byte-identical).
460-    junction_dominance_filter: bool = False
461-    junction_dominance_min_reads: int = 2
462-    junction_dominance_window_bp: int = 20
463-    junction_dominance_tol_bp: int = 2
464-    # M2/M1 read-support gate (quant_mode="m2_em" only). A multi-exon candidate
465:    # (GTF or novel; fusion/mono exempt) is KEPT iff it earns >=1 read's support:
466-    #   (a) it is some read's M1 SOLE best-AS (the read's tie set is exactly this
467-    #       candidate -> unique mappy-AS winner), OR
468-    #   (b) it is some read's M2 best (lowest junction-NLL among that read's tie).
469-    # Otherwise it is dropped. Rationale: a jitter-corrupted GTF junction loses the
470-    # M2 signal contest to the true-junction candidate AND never wins a read's sole
471-    # M1-AS, so it earns no support; a genuine isoform always wins either its own
472-    # sole-AS reads or the M2 contest. Catches corrupted-annotation shadows the
473-    # abundance levers miss. OFF == no drops. m2_support_gate_tie: when True (default)
474-    # a candidate tied for the lowest M2 NLL also counts as M2-best (recall-safer);
475-    # False requires a strict unique M2 win.
476-    # Default ON (tie-accept) tuned on the SGNex heya8+sirv4 12-condition matrix:
477-    # lifts the corrupted-annotation (c_jitter) F1@3 with ZERO recall loss anywhere
478-    # (full Sn@3 stays 100). The strict (tie=False) variant drops more shadows but
479-    # costs full-condition recall (Sn@3 -> 98.8), so tie-accept is the default.
480-    m2_support_gate: bool = True
481-    m2_support_gate_tie: bool = True
482-    # Score-gated fallback split (mk1). On a tie that M2 does NOT win
483-    # outright (margin < m2_tiebreak_margin), restrict the 1/K split to the
484-    # candidates eventalign could actually score in the junction window; the
485-    # candidates with NO window signal are excluded for THIS read. If NONE
486-    # scored, keep the full 1/K split over all tied candidates (== M1). The read
487-    # is never dropped, so this is recall-safe. On SIRV it is a no-op (only ~2
488-    # "mixed" reads; verified gffcompare == baseline); kept ON as the principled
489-    # per-read rule that may matter on real dRNA. Configurable.
490-    m2_tie_scoregate_split: bool = True
491-
492-    # EM prior / scoring. VESTIGIAL: the combined_score-derived EM prior was driven
493-    # by the composite scorer, which has been removed; the assembly runner never

495-    score_alpha: float = 0.5          # (inert) weight for coherence vs discrimination in combined_score
496-    prior_weight_cap: float = 10.0    # (inert) max multiplicative boost from prior
497-    use_prior: bool = True            # (inert) no code path applies a prior in assembly

137-def isoform_fraction_drops(
138-    results: Dict[str, QuantResult],
139-    min_fraction: float,
140-) -> set:
141-    """Return candidate_ids to drop by the minimum-isoform-fraction heuristic.
142-
143-    For every NOVEL multi-exon transcript C, compute its locus-relative
144-    abundance::
145-
146-        relabund(C) = C.abundance / max(abundance over ALL transcripts
147-                       (novel, GTF and fusion) that OVERLAP C, C included)
148-
149-    and drop C when ``relabund(C) < min_fraction``. This is the standard
150-    Cufflinks ``--min-isoform-fraction`` / StringTie ``-f`` minor-isoform
151-    suppression rule: the low-fraction tail of a locus is enriched for
152-    incompletely-spliced precursors (pre-mRNA), RT/template-switching
153-    artifacts, and assembly noise rather than genuine isoforms.
154-
155-    The locus maximum spans ALL sources (not just novel): on dense annotated
156-    loci the dominant isoform is usually a GTF-passthrough transcript, so
157-    comparing a novel only against other novels lets wobble-shadow isoforms
158-    (junction-shifted near-copies of a true transcript) escape — they only
159-    compete with each other and keep a deceptively high novel-only fraction.
160-    Measuring each novel against the strongest transcript of ANY source at its
161-    locus collapses those shadows below the threshold.
162-
163-    GTF-passthrough (``source="gtf"``), fusion (``source="fusion"``), and
164:    single-exon (mono) candidates are EXEMPT and never dropped — they only ever
165-    contribute to the locus maximum, never to the drop set. ``min_fraction
166-    <= 0`` disables the filter (returns an empty set).
167-
168-    SIRV WARNING: synthetic SIRV has no genuine low-abundance isoform tail, so
169-    its F1-optimal threshold (~0.4) is 4-40x more aggressive than real tools
170-    and would gut recall on a real transcriptome. Keep this conservative
171-    (literature default 0.01) and re-tune on real data.
172-    """
173-    if min_fraction <= 0.0:
174-        return set()
175-    # Bucket by (chrom, strand): only candidates on the same chromosome and
176-    # strand can share a locus, so the pairwise overlap scan stays within a
177-    # bucket instead of running all-vs-all across the whole genome. (Within a
178-    # locus-dense single chrom/strand it is still O(n^2); a sweep-line could be
179-    # added if a real run ever makes that the bottleneck.)
180-    #
181-    # The bucket holds ALL candidates (so the locus maximum can come from a GTF
182-    # or fusion transcript); only NOVEL multi-exon candidates are eligible to be
183-    # dropped.
184-    buckets: Dict[Tuple[str, str], List[QuantResult]] = defaultdict(list)
185-    for qr in results.values():
186-        buckets[(qr.chrom, qr.strand)].append(qr)
187-    drops: set = set()
188-    for bucket in buckets.values():
189-        for qr in bucket:
190:            # Only novel multi-exon candidates are droppable; GTF/fusion/mono
191-            # only ever serve as locus-maximum competitors.
192-            if qr.source != "novel" or len(qr.exons) < 2:
193-                continue
194-            locus_max = qr.abundance
195-            for other in bucket:
196-                if other.candidate_id != qr.candidate_id and _qr_overlap(qr, other):
197-                    if other.abundance > locus_max:
198-                        locus_max = other.abundance
199-            # The locus-dominant isoform (relabund == 1.0) is NEVER dropped,
200-            # even if a caller mis-configures min_fraction > 1.0.
201-            if qr.abundance >= locus_max:
202-                continue
203-            if locus_max > 0 and (qr.abundance / locus_max) < min_fraction:
204-                drops.add(qr.candidate_id)
205-    return drops

208-def soft_mass_ratio_drops(
209-    results: Dict[str, QuantResult],
210-    max_ratio: float,
211-) -> set:
212-    """Return candidate_ids to drop by the soft-mass / hard-read ratio test.
213-
214-    For a NOVEL multi-exon candidate C, compare its EM SOFT abundance
215-    (``R.sum`` = fractional probability mass) against its HARD read count
216-    (``num_assigned_reads`` = number of reads whose argmax is C)::
217-
218-        ratio(C) = C.abundance / C.num_assigned_reads
219-
220-    and drop C when ``ratio >= max_ratio`` (a candidate with zero hard reads is
221-    always dropped — it lives entirely on borrowed soft mass).
222-
223-    A genuine isoform's reads argmax to it AND deposit their full soft mass on
224-    it, so ratio ~= 1. A wobble shadow steals little hard support but, being a
225-    +-bp structural near-copy of a high-abundance anchor, accumulates fractional
226-    soft crumbs from every one of the anchor's reads -> its soft abundance is
227-    inflated far above its honest hard-read count (ratio >> 1). This catches the
228-    HIGH-abundance shadows that the locus-relative ``isoform_fraction`` /
229-    cluster-recheck levers cannot (a shadow at 32% of the anchor passes a
230-    fraction gate but still shows an inflated soft/hard ratio). Pure EM evidence
231-    — no GTF, no annotation oracle.
232-
233-    GTF-passthrough (``source="gtf"``), fusion (``source="fusion"``), and
234:    single-exon (mono) candidates are EXEMPT. ``max_ratio <= 0`` disables the
235-    filter (returns an empty set).
236-
237-    SIRV WARNING: the separating threshold is data-dependent (true isoforms
238-    cluster at ratio ~1 but a few reach ~1.4-1.6 when their own reads are
239-    genuinely ambiguous); tune on real data before trusting an aggressive value.
240-    """
241-    if max_ratio <= 0.0:
242-        return set()
243-    drops: set = set()
244-    for qr in results.values():
245-        if qr.source != "novel" or len(qr.exons) < 2:
246-            continue
247-        if qr.num_assigned_reads <= 0:
248-            drops.add(qr.candidate_id)
249-            continue
250-        if (qr.abundance / qr.num_assigned_reads) >= max_ratio:
251-            drops.add(qr.candidate_id)
252-    return drops

fin/pipeline/selection.py
1-"""Selection layer: decide which candidates survive, in one place.
2-
3-Historically "which candidates are real" had no single home — it was smeared across three
4-incompatible mechanisms: pre-EM gates mutating the candidate list, a post-EM ``drop_cols`` column
5-set buried inside ``_quant_m2_em``, and finalize-time dict deletion. This module is the single home
6-for the post-EM INTERVAL scope (M3 Step 1): the cascade that runs after assignment, within one
7-interval, over the ``QuantOutput`` of the m2_em Assigner.
8-
9-Design (behavior-preserving; see the pipeline reorganization plan + Codex review 2):
10-- ``SelectionOutcome`` is the uniform record of every keep/drop/fold decision (free FN/FP-attribution
11-  provenance, replacing the ad-hoc ``logger.info`` drop counters).
12-- ``select_m2_interval`` reproduces the CURRENT sequential cascade EXACTLY — each gate evaluates
13-  against the survivor set left by the earlier gates (``exclude=drop_cols``), the two folds
14-  (containment-collapse, mono-resolve) move reads/mass into their parent before the final apply, and
15-  ordering is load-bearing (it is the byte-identity guarantee). The cleaner "evaluate all on one base
16-  snapshot + declared precedence" model is a separate, behavior-CHANGING follow-up, not this step.
17-
18-Scope: the DEFAULT ``m2_em`` path only. ``argmax`` / ``m1_em`` / ``cluster`` keep their own survival
19-handling. The PRE_ASSIGN (canonical / junction-dominance) and GLOBAL (finalize filters) scopes move
20-here in later M3 steps.
21-"""

codex
No code changes made.

**Decision 1**
Agree. Folding should leave `cluster_read_chains`.

The current clustering function is doing irreversible candidate collapse before the downstream model has evidence: exact sub-chain fold happens before union-find at [chain_cluster.py](/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin/fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py:220), span-guard logic is part of that fold at [chain_cluster.py](/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin/fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py:241), and mono containment fold is another assignment decision at [chain_cluster.py](/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin/fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py:264). Those are not clustering operations. They change candidate existence and read ownership.

No structural reason fold must stay at cluster time. The only reasons are pragmatic: candidate-count reduction and preserving shadow provenance for 5-prime recovery. Both can be handled by a later named per-cluster collapse/recovery step. In fact, config comments already say the exact sub-chain collapse accounts for most of the observed recall cost, so making it an explicit later decision is cleaner.

**Decision 2**
Agree. Keep single-linkage for now.

If a cluster is only a read-pool scope and all structural variants remain candidates, over-clustering is usually lower harm than under-clustering. Under-clustering can split reads that should compete once; over-clustering mostly adds extra competitors, and M1/M2/EM should assign away from bad matches.

I would not switch to complete-linkage or representative-centroid now. Complete-linkage will split real local neighborhoods where A and C are not directly within tolerance but both share reads through B. Representative methods are order/rep-sensitive unless you add more policy.

Where single-linkage can bite:

- Bridge artifacts: `A` shares upstream structure with `B`, `B` shares downstream structure with `C`, but `A` and `C` are separate loci.
- Large same-interval components from noisy chimeric/read-through chains.
- Any later step that uses cluster-level max abundance or cluster-level collapse as a truth decision rather than just as assignment scope.
- Runtime: one bridge can turn many small assignment problems into one large one.

Cheap better proposal: keep union-find, but add diagnostics/guards later: log component size, span width, max pairwise wobble drift, and support of bridge chains. If this becomes a real failure mode, split only obvious pathological components, such as disconnected genomic span groups or components connected solely by one-read bridge chains.

**Decision 3**
Mostly agree, but with one important refinement: the mono bucket must be metadata, not a candidate.

(a) Deferring mono resolution is correct in principle. You cannot reliably decide whether a chain-less read is a real intronless transcript or a degradation fragment until you know the surviving multi-exon exon coordinates. This matches the intent of `mono_resolve_post_em`: runner already disables generation-time mono fold when post-EM mono resolution is enabled at [runner.py](/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin/fin/pipeline/runner.py:183), and selection resolves mono after interval structural gates at [selection.py](/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin/fin/pipeline/selection.py:284).

But current implementation is only halfway there. It still emits mono candidates before EM, lets them participate in EM, then post-processes their hard-assigned reads in [mono_resolve_drops](/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin/fin/scoring/m2_junction_nll.py:741). The proposed design, where mono reads are held out until final multi resolution, is cleaner.

(b) One interval-level mono bucket is fine as a holding bucket only. It must not become one transcript with one giant span. The final mono step needs to split residual mono reads into spatial loci, like the existing `_spatial_read_clusters` helper does at [discovery.py](/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin/fin/candidates/discovery.py:37), then emit/drop per locus.

Recall protection for real intronless genes needs locus-level evidence:

- Keep uncovered mono loci if they meet conservative read-support and length floors.
- Keep or favor known single-exon GTF overlaps when annotation exists.
- Use strand-aware exonic containment against surviving multi candidates.
- Do not fold reads that overlap a candidate intron.
- Treat polyA/5-prime endpoint coherence as supporting evidence, not a universal requirement, because some real intronless transcripts are not safely polyA-positive.
- Be careful with real same-strand intronless genes fully inside a multi-exon exon. Geometry alone cannot distinguish these from fragments. If such a mono locus has strong coherent endpoints/support, it should survive even if contained.

For mono reads contained in several surviving multi candidates, hard-assigning to the highest abundance is acceptable for a first version, but biased. The better abundance model is fractional assignment across covering multis, proportional to final multi abundance, with unique cover getting weight 1.0.

(c) Holding mono reads out of multi-exon EM is better for structural resolution. A read wholly inside a constitutive exon usually cannot discriminate isoforms, so including it in EM can swamp junction evidence and distort abundance ratios used by post-EM gates. Holding it out removes that noise.

The cost is that some mono reads do carry useful expression signal, especially if they land in an exon unique to one surviving isoform. That signal should be added after structural survival is decided. Post-hoc addition is sound for reported abundance if mass is conserved and ambiguous covers are weighted. It is less sound if you add `+1` to one highest-abundance candidate for every multi-cover read; that is what the current resolver does at [m2_junction_nll.py](/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin/fin/scoring/m2_junction_nll.py:795), and it is a pragmatic prototype, not the ideal final weighted model.

(d) Better lifecycle:

1. Clustering: multi-exon chains only; preserve all chain variants; store all chain-less reads in an interval mono bucket.
2. Multi resolution: run EM/selection on multi candidates using spliced/multi evidence.
3. Mono finalizer: with the final surviving multi set, classify each mono read/locus as folded-to-multi, residual genuine mono, or dropped noise.
4. Abundance/output: add folded mono mass before reporting abundance/TPM, and ensure no later gate can drop a multi target after mono reads have been folded into it.

Bottom line: Decisions 1 and 2 are solid. Decision 3 is the right direction, but I would make the final mono stage locus-aware and weight-aware; otherwise it will protect precision but risk both intronless recall and biased abundance.

Under-clustering can split reads that should compete once; over-clustering mostly adds extra competitors, and M1/M2/EM should assign away from bad matches.

I would not switch to complete-linkage or representative-centroid now. Complete-linkage will split real local neighborhoods where A and C are not directly within tolerance but both share reads through B. Representative methods are order/rep-sensitive unless you add more policy.

Where single-linkage can bite:

- Bridge artifacts: `A` shares upstream structure with `B`, `B` shares downstream structure with `C`, but `A` and `C` are separate loci.
- Large same-interval components from noisy chimeric/read-through chains.
- Any later step that uses cluster-level max abundance or cluster-level collapse as a truth decision rather than just as assignment scope.
- Runtime: one bridge can turn many small assignment problems into one large one.

Cheap better proposal: keep union-find, but add diagnostics/guards later: log component size, span width, max pairwise wobble drift, and support of bridge chains. If this becomes a real failure mode, split only obvious pathological components, such as disconnected genomic span groups or components connected solely by one-read bridge chains.

**Decision 3**
Mostly agree, but with one important refinement: the mono bucket must be metadata, not a candidate.

(a) Deferring mono resolution is correct in principle. You cannot reliably decide whether a chain-less read is a real intronless transcript or a degradation fragment until you know the surviving multi-exon exon coordinates. This matches the intent of `mono_resolve_post_em`: runner already disables generation-time mono fold when post-EM mono resolution is enabled at [runner.py](/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin/fin/pipeline/runner.py:183), and selection resolves mono after interval structural gates at [selection.py](/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin/fin/pipeline/selection.py:284).

But current implementation is only halfway there. It still emits mono candidates before EM, lets them participate in EM, then post-processes their hard-assigned reads in [mono_resolve_drops](/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin/fin/scoring/m2_junction_nll.py:741). The proposed design, where mono reads are held out until final multi resolution, is cleaner.

(b) One interval-level mono bucket is fine as a holding bucket only. It must not become one transcript with one giant span. The final mono step needs to split residual mono reads into spatial loci, like the existing `_spatial_read_clusters` helper does at [discovery.py](/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin/fin/candidates/discovery.py:37), then emit/drop per locus.

Recall protection for real intronless genes needs locus-level evidence:

- Keep uncovered mono loci if they meet conservative read-support and length floors.
- Keep or favor known single-exon GTF overlaps when annotation exists.
- Use strand-aware exonic containment against surviving multi candidates.
- Do not fold reads that overlap a candidate intron.
- Treat polyA/5-prime endpoint coherence as supporting evidence, not a universal requirement, because some real intronless transcripts are not safely polyA-positive.
- Be careful with real same-strand intronless genes fully inside a multi-exon exon. Geometry alone cannot distinguish these from fragments. If such a mono locus has strong coherent endpoints/support, it should survive even if contained.

For mono reads contained in several surviving multi candidates, hard-assigning to the highest abundance is acceptable for a first version, but biased. The better abundance model is fractional assignment across covering multis, proportional to final multi abundance, with unique cover getting weight 1.0.

(c) Holding mono reads out of multi-exon EM is better for structural resolution. A read wholly inside a constitutive exon usually cannot discriminate isoforms, so including it in EM can swamp junction evidence and distort abundance ratios used by post-EM gates. Holding it out removes that noise.

The cost is that some mono reads do carry useful expression signal, especially if they land in an exon unique to one surviving isoform. That signal should be added after structural survival is decided. Post-hoc addition is sound for reported abundance if mass is conserved and ambiguous covers are weighted. It is less sound if you add `+1` to one highest-abundance candidate for every multi-cover read; that is what the current resolver does at [m2_junction_nll.py](/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin/fin/scoring/m2_junction_nll.py:795), and it is a pragmatic prototype, not the ideal final weighted model.

(d) Better lifecycle:

1. Clustering: multi-exon chains only; preserve all chain variants; store all chain-less reads in an interval mono bucket.
2. Multi resolution: run EM/selection on multi candidates using spliced/multi evidence.
3. Mono finalizer: with the final surviving multi set, classify each mono read/locus as folded-to-multi, residual genuine mono, or dropped noise.
4. Abundance/output: add folded mono mass before reporting abundance/TPM, and ensure no later gate can drop a multi target after mono reads have been folded into it.

Bottom line: Decisions 1 and 2 are solid. Decision 3 is the right direction, but I would make the final mono stage locus-aware and weight-aware; otherwise it will protect precision but risk both intronless recall and biased abundance.
